<TD NOWRAP VALIGN="bottom">&nbsp;</TD>

<TD NOWRAP VALIGN="bottom">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD NOWRAP VALIGN="bottom" ALIGN="right">-26</TD>
<TD NOWRAP VALIGN="bottom">%&nbsp;</TD></TR>
</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In US$ million, unless noted otherwise. EBITDA = Operating income + Depreciation of PP&amp;E + Depreciation of the right of
use assets + Amortization of intangible assets + Unproductive exploratory drillings + (Reversal) / Deterioration of PP&amp;E. Adjusted EBITDA = EBITDA that excludes IFRS 16 effects +/- <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">one-off</FONT> items. Net
Leverage Ratio = Net Debt / LTM Adj. EBITDA. FCF = Cash flow from Operations less CAPEX (Investing activities), M&amp;A (Investing activities), and interest and leasing payments (Financing activities). Fuels = diesel + gasoline. R&amp;M is refining
and marketing business, it excludes petrochemicals and agro products. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adj. EBITDA</B> totaled US$1,357&nbsp;million (+21% q/q), mainly driven by
higher shale oil production, lower lifting costs related to the divestment of mature fields, higher seasonal sales of natural gas, in addition to record processing level at our refineries, partially offset by a slight contraction in local fuel
prices based on a very volatile environment. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">On a y/y basis, EBITDA remained essentially flat, despite a 13% interannual contraction in Brent&#8217;s
price. The lower fuels prices were almost offset by higher shale oil production and lower lifting costs on the back of reduced exposure to mature fields. Moreover, processing levels stood 9% above last year. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX </B>amounted to US$1,017&nbsp;million, 12% below the previous quarter, mostly driven by lower cost in USD terms, from which 70% was allocated to the
unconventional business, reaffirming our focus on Vaca Muerta. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Shale oil production</B> continued delivering an <B>impressive growth rate of 35%
y/y</B> averaging 170 kbbl/d (+17% q/q), representing 71% of our total oil production (2Q25: 59% and 3Q24: 49%), almost offsetting the decline in mature fields. Excluding the effect of the sale of Aguada del Cha&ntilde;ar block, <B>shale oil
production would have increased by 43% y/y.</B> </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Lifting costs</B> significantly dropped to 8.8 US$/boe <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-28%</FONT>
q/q and <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-45%</FONT> y/y), as the result of the successful exit strategy from conventional mature fields and increased shale production. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Processing levels at our refineries </B>averaged 326 kbbl/d, record high since 2009, representing 97% of refineries&#8217; utilization rate, mainly driven
by higher processing levels at La Plata refinery that was awarded as the Refinery of the Year in Latin America by LARTC.<B> </B></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Progress on our main
projects: </B></P> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">&#149;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B>VMOS</B>: (construction progress ~35% as of <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Sep-25)</FONT> during the
quarter, works related to the oil pipeline routes and trench excavation were completed, on track with the plan. Moreover, in early <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Nov-25</FONT> welding works for the 440 km oil pipeline were completed.
</P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">&#149;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B>Argentina LNG</B>: in <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Oct-25</FONT> YPF and ENI signed the technical FID for
Phase 3 (~12 MTPA, expandable to ~18 MTPA). Moreover, in <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Nov-25,</FONT> ADNOC, through XRG, its investment subsidiary, signed a preliminary framework agreement with YPF and ENI, aiming to join the Argentina LNG
Project. </P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Free cash flow </B>totaled a negative US$759&nbsp;million in 3Q25, as expected, mostly explained by the extraordinary
effects related to the recent acquisition of shale assets (US$523 million) and the impact of the mature fields exit strategy. As a result, net debt increased to 9.6&nbsp;billion dollars, pushing our <B>net leverage ratio</B> up to 2.1x. Excluding
the recent acquisition of shale assets, the net leverage ratio proforma would have amounted to 1.9x. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">On the <B>financial front</B>, in October, YPF
reopened the syndicated corporate cross-border loan market for US$700&nbsp;million and retaped the 2031 international bond for US$500&nbsp;million at 8.25% yield. </P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net Revenues</B> amounted to US$4,643&nbsp;million, flat against 2Q25, mainly due to increased fuel demand and peak natural
gas sales during the winter, offset by lower gasoline and diesel prices. Moreover, Medanito oil exports volumes increased by 14% q/q, partially compensated by the extraordinary export of Escalante oil in 2Q25. Finally, grain and flour sales declined
in 3Q25 due to lower seasonal demand. </P> <P STYLE="font-size:12pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Stock variations include price effects by (US$ 4) million in 3Q25, (US$96) million for 2Q25, US$94 million for 3Q24, (US$96)
million for 9M25 and (US$64) million for 9M24 </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>OPEX</B> totaled US$1,356&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-11%</FONT> q/q), mainly
derived from savings associated with the reduced exposure to conventional mature fields, significant <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">ramp-up</FONT> in efficient shale production and lower costs in dollar terms, partially offset by higher <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">non-cash</FONT> environmental provisions recorded in Other Upstream costs. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Total Other Costs</B> reached
US$1,358&nbsp;million (+1% q/q), primarily explained by higher depreciation and amortization related to increased shale activity, partially offset by lower oil export duties and royalties from mature fields. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Purchases</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;&amp; Stock Variations</B> amounted to US$1,324&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-1%</FONT> q/q).
<B>Purchases</B> increased q/q, mostly driven by higher oil purchases to third parties as the result of the reduced exposure to mature fields and higher processing levels at refineries. <B>Stock variations</B> reached a positive charge of
US$54&nbsp;million in 3Q25 (compared to a negative US$132&nbsp;million in 2Q25), primarily on the back of higher crude oil purchases to third parties to restock inventories and compensate the inventory drawdown recorded in 2Q25. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Other operating net results</B> totaled negative US$48&nbsp;million (vs. a loss of US$26&nbsp;million in 2Q25). In 2Q25, the provision of losses related to
mature fields were partially offset by the positive result from the sale of mature fields and 49% of Aguada del Cha&ntilde;ar block, as well as revaluation of companies. In 3Q25, we recorded lower provision of losses from mature fields.
</P><DIV STYLE="line-height:8.0pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:2pt;border-bottom:1px solid #000000;width:11%">&nbsp;</DIV>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Financial net results</B> recorded a loss of US$245&nbsp;million, 4.5% below the previous quarter, primarily explained by a
decrease in the <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap"><FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">mark-to-market</FONT></FONT> valuation of sovereign bonds within the cash position, offset by a lower abandonment provision following the exit from mature fields. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Income tax </B>recorded a negative charge of US$537&nbsp;million, versus a negative charge of US$92&nbsp;million in 2Q25, mostly reflecting higher <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">non-cash</FONT> deferred income tax. Consequently, <B>net result</B> totaled a loss of US$198&nbsp;million. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>2. ADJ.

<P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:1pt; border-bottom:1.00pt solid #000000; display:table-cell; font-size:8pt; font-family:Times New Roman; "><B>Unaudited Figures, in US$ million</B></P></TD>

<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD>&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD>&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom">&nbsp;&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD>&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom">&nbsp;&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD>&nbsp;</TD></TR>
</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Revenues</B> totaled US$1,967&nbsp;million (+4% q/q), mainly driven by higher seasonal sales of natural gas (+6% price and
+6% volume) coupled with increased shale production, partially counterbalanced by lower conventional production. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Lifting costs</B> dropped to
US$8.8/BOE <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-28%</FONT> q/q), reflecting the considerable progress in the divestment of mature fields, coupled by shale oil <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">ramp-up.</FONT> When breaking down our lifting costs by
type of operation in 3Q25, our unconventional activities averaged 4.2 US$/BOE, remaining almost flat q/q, while our conventional operations averaged 20.8 US$/BOE, representing a sequential decrease of 23% mainly driven by the evolution of the
divestment in mature fields. Lifting cost gross within our shale core hub averaged 4.6 US$/BOE in 3Q25, decreasing 7% q/q, as 2Q25 was affected by higher pulling and maintenance costs. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Royalties </B>amounted to US$237&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-2%</FONT> q/q), primarily driven by lower mature fields production,
partially offset by shale production <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">ramp-up</FONT> and higher natural gas prices. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Other costs</B> amounted to
US$274&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-2%</FONT> q/q), mainly explained by lower <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">one-off</FONT> costs related to mature fields in 3Q25, partially compensated by the positive results from sale of 49%
of Aguada del Cha&ntilde;ar booked in 2Q25. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adj. EBITDA</B> totaled US$1,042&nbsp;million (+35% q/q), mostly boosted by better lifting costs (exiting
mature fields and expanding shale) and higher seasonal sales of natural gas, and to a minor extent, lower operating costs in dollar terms. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX</B>
amounted to US$751&nbsp;million, from which 94% was allocated to unconventional assets, mostly destined to drilling and workover activities. The sequential 13% decrease is the result of lower costs in dollar terms. </P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="right">Page 5/14 </P>

</DIV></Center>


<p style="margin-top:1em; margin-bottom:0em; page-break-before:always"> </p>
<HR SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">


<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">


<IMG SRC="g66804dsp008a.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Unconventional horizontal oil wells</B> recorded again strong metrics, particularly <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">tied-in</FONT> activities: </P> <P STYLE="font-size:12pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt" ALIGN="center">


<IMG SRC="g66804dsp008b.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In terms of <B>efficiencies within our unconventional operations</B>, we achieved solid operational metrics in terms of
drilling and fracking activities. In this sense, we averaged 337 meters/day of drilling speed in our <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">core-hub</FONT> blocks (+2% q/q), and 279 <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">stages/set-month</FONT> of fracking
speed (+8% q/q). </P> <P STYLE="font-size:12pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Crude oil production</B> averaged 240 kbbl/d <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-3%</FONT> q/q), mainly explained by lower
conventional production from mature fields, mostly replaced by the notable <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">ramp-up</FONT> in shale oil production (+17% q/q), highlighting the increased shale oil production within our
<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">core-hub</FONT> blocks (+12%) and La Angostura Sur (+37%), among others. On an interannual basis, shale oil production recorded an impressive growth rate of 35%, and when excluding the effect of the sale of Aguada
del Cha&ntilde;ar block, <B>shale oil production would have increased by 43% y/y.</B> </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Natural gas production </B>contracted by <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-3%</FONT> q/q, primarily explained by lower output from mature fields, slightly offset by increased shale gas production, especially in La Calera and Aguada de la Arena wet gas blocks. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>NGLs production</B> also dropped <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-13%</FONT> q/q, primarily due to operational issues at La Calera block (normalized in
October), and lower contribution from mature fields. </P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="right">Page 6/14 </P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Stock variations include price effects by (US$54) million in 3Q24, (US$48) million for 3Q25, (US$60) million for 2Q25,
(US$100) million for 9M25 and US$86 million for 9M24. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Revenues</B> totaled US$3.7&nbsp;billion <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-1%</FONT> q/q)
mainly as a result of lower prices of local fuels and the basket of refined products other than gasoline and diesel partially compensated by an increase in volumes dispatched of diesel and gasoline in the local market, higher exports of gasoline and
jet fuel to neighboring countries and greater demand of fertilizers in the local market. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>OPEX Downstream</B> amounted to US$520&nbsp;million <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-1%</FONT> q/q), mainly due to lower costs in dollar terms and lower maintenance costs as the 2Q25 was affected by programmed stoppage in La Plata refinery. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Fuel imports </B>reached US$35&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-33%</FONT> q/q), driven by higher production of gasoline and diesel in our
refineries. In that sense, in 3Q25, fuel imports remained at very low levels, representing only 1% of total fuel sales, compared to 3% in 2Q25 and 4% in 3Q24. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Crude oil purchases </B>(intersegment + third parties) amounted to US$2,012&nbsp;million (+14% q/q), driven by increased processing levels, given the
record achieved in 3Q25, while 2Q25 was affected by the maintenance stoppage at La Plata Refinery. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Biofuel purchases </B>decreased by 15% q/q, where
purchases of biodiesel declined 22%, and purchases of bioethanol dropped 6%. Biodiesel purchases contraction was mainly the result of the lower blend in diesel sales, the latter due to supply constraints in the local market, while bioethanol
purchases decreased mainly as a result of lower prices, partially offset by higher volumes aligned to greater gasoline demand. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Agro products purchases
</B>(+1% q/q): mostly in line with higher local sales of fertilizers, partially offset by lower exports of grain and flour. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Stock variations</B>
totaled a positive charge of US$35&nbsp;million (vs. a negative charge of US$114&nbsp;million in 2Q25), primarily on the back of higher crude oil purchases to third parties to restock inventories and compensate the inventory drawdown recorded in
2Q25. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Other costs </B>increased by +12% q/q, mostly due to the positive result from the revaluation of Loma Campana &#8211; Lago Pellegrini pipeline
booked in 2Q25 after completing its acquisition, slightly offset by lower costs in dollar terms. </P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="right">Page 7/14 </P>

</DIV></Center>


<p style="margin-top:1em; margin-bottom:0em; page-break-before:always"> </p>
<HR SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">


<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">


<IMG SRC="g66804dsp008a.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adj. EBITDA,</B> excluding inventories price effect of oil products, totaled US$354&nbsp;million <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-19%</FONT> q/q), mainly driven by lower local fuel prices, partially offset by higher processing levels and lower fuel imports. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adj. EBITDA of the Refining</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;&amp; Marketing</B> business, in unit terms, amounted to US$5.9/bbl, below the Adjusted EBITDA recorded in
2Q25 of US$11.9/bbl. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX</B> amounted to US$218&nbsp;million in 3Q25, where 68% was allocated to refining, 14% to midstream (O&amp;G), 13% to
logistics and 5% others. The sequential drop of 11% is mostly driven by lower costs in dollar terms. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In our <B>refineries,</B> CAPEX was mostly allocated
to the following main projects: </P> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">&#149;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B>New fuel specifications project</B>, to comply with the Resolution No.&nbsp;492/2023 of the Secretary of
Energy. In that sense, the construction of a new diesel oil hydrotreatment unit at Luj&aacute;n de Cuyo refinery continued making progress, expecting to be operational by June 2026. </P></TD></TR></TABLE>
<P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">&#149;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B>Revamping of topping units</B> at Luj&aacute;n de Cuyo refinery. We continue making progress in the
reconditioning of the refinery to process lighter Vaca Muerta&#8217;s shale oil, expected to be in place in 1H26. </P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In our <B>midstream
oil</B> business unit, our affiliate VMOS continued moving forward with its project: </P> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">&#149;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B>VMOS</B>: (construction progress ~35% as of <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Sep-25)</FONT> during the
quarter, works related to the oil pipeline routes and trench excavation were completed, on track with the plan. Moreover, in early <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Nov-25</FONT> welding works for the 440 km oil pipeline were completed.
</P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In our <B>midstream gas</B> business unit, we also continued making progress on our main projects: </P>
<P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">&#149;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B></B>Fully Revamping of the natural <B>gas treatment plant at Loma La Lata</B>, increasing its current
capacity, and improving the treatment of associated gas. The revamping is expected to be operational in 1Q26. </P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">

<TD WIDTH="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">&#149;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B>South Hub gathering project</B> to expand gas processing capacity at Sierra Barrosa treatment plants. The
first phase was completed in 2024, while second phase is expected to be completed in 2027. </P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">

<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B>North Hub gathering project</B>, consistent in the construction of a new gas pipeline connecting Narambuena
and Bajo del Toro blocks with El Port&oacute;n Industrial Complex. Early <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">start-up</FONT> is expected by 2Q26 to become fully operational in 2027. </P></TD></TR></TABLE>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Net Average domestic prices for gasoline and diesel are net of taxes, commissions, commercial bonuses and freights. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Crude oil processed </B>averaged 326 kbbl/d (+8% q/q), reaching a record-high since 2009 coupled with an utilization rate of 97%, mainly boosted by the
solid performance recorded in La Plata refinery in 3Q25, which processed 196 kbbl/d (+14% q/q), while 2Q25 was affected by programmed maintenances. </P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="right">Page 8/14 </P>

</DIV></Center>


<p style="margin-top:1em; margin-bottom:0em; page-break-before:always"> </p>
<HR SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">


<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">


<IMG SRC="g66804dsp008a.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Domestic fuels sales volumes</B> reached 3,655 km3, growing +3% q/q (gasoline +6% and diesel +2%), on the
back of higher market share in the retail and industrial segments, slightly offset by lower seasonal diesel sales to the agricultural segment. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Petrochemicals sales volumes</B> decreased by 10% q/q, mainly due to lower exports of methanol, partially offset by higher local demand.
<B>Fertilizers&#8217; local sales volume </B>grew by 64% q/q, primary on the back of increased market share and presales. While <B>grain and flour sales volumes</B> dropped 22% q/q, mostly due to lower seasonal exports, but continued boosted by the
temporary elimination of export duties until <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Oct-25.</FONT> </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net average fuels prices in local market measured in
dollar terms </B>dropped 5% q/q, mainly due to a very volatile local scenario, representing a temporary gap against import parity of around 10%, which started normalizing during October. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Prices for petrochemical</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;&amp; other refined products</B> increased by 11% q/q, mainly due to upward trend in international prices of
petrochemical and certain refined products, primarily lubricant bases and coal. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>3.3 LNG&nbsp;&amp; INTEGRATED GAS </B></P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adj. EBITDA</B> totaled negative US$4&nbsp;million, compared to slightly negative US$0.1&nbsp;million in 2Q25. In 3Q25
natural gas sales grew sequentially due to higher winter seasonal demand, in line with the increase in natural gas purchases. However, operating costs increased against the previous quarter mostly driven by higher activity related to Argentina LNG
Project. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX</B> amounted to US$9&nbsp;million in 3Q25, mostly allocated to the Argentina LNG Project, which continued progressing during the
quarter throughout its 3 phases. Regarding phase 3, in October, YPF and ENI signed a technical FID, targeting a project with a total capacity of ~12 MTPA, expandable to ~18 MTPA, and in November, ADNOC signed a preliminary framework agreement with
YPF and ENI, aiming to join the Argentina LNG Project. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>3.4 NEW ENERGIES </B></P> <P STYLE="font-size:12pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

<HR SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">


<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">


<IMG SRC="g66804dsp008a.jpg" ALT="LOGO" STYLE="width:8.32986in;height:0.444444in;">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adj. EBITDA </B>totaled US$52&nbsp;million, an improvement of $27&nbsp;million sequentially, almost
entirely attributable to Metrogas subsidiary, as a result of higher sales to residential customers due to increased winter demand, partially offset by lower price in dollar terms. </P>
<P STYLE="font-size:18pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="4%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left"><B>4.</B></TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman; " ALIGN="left"><B>LIQUIDITY AND SOURCES OF CAPITAL </B></P></TD></TR></TABLE>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>4.1 CASH FLOW SUMMARY </B></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Despite 3Q25 adjusted EBITDA
surpassed CAPEX deployment and regular interest payment, <B>free cash flow</B> reached a negative US$759&nbsp;million, mainly due to the acquisition of shale assets from Total Austral S.A. for
<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-US$523&nbsp;million</FONT> and a negative working capital of <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-US$359&nbsp;million</FONT> mostly associated with the discontinued operations in our mature fields, income tax payment
from our subsidiaries and longer collection days from natural gas clients and Plan Gas program that started to normalize during October. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Excluding the
M&amp;A transaction with Total and <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">one-off</FONT> items related to mature fields, negative free cash flow proforma would have been <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-US$172&nbsp;million.</FONT> </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In terms of liquidity, our <B>cash and short-term investments</B> slightly increased to US$1,016&nbsp;million by the end of September 2025 (+0.5% q/q). </P>
<P STYLE="font-size:18pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt" ALIGN="center">


<IMG SRC="g66804dsp012.jpg" ALT="LOGO" STYLE="width:5.07431in;height:2.04653in;">
 </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Notes: (1)&nbsp;Approximation of cash flow evolution, highlighting key figures. Cash&nbsp;&amp; equivalents include
Argentine sovereign bonds and Treasury notes. (2)&nbsp;Refers to the acquisition of La Escalonada&nbsp;&amp; Rinc&oacute;n la Ceniza blocks. (3)&nbsp;Others mainly include mature fields <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">one-off</FONT> items for a
total of <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-13):</FONT> operating optimizations <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-11),</FONT> severance indemnities <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-3),</FONT> additions of assets held for sale <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-3),</FONT> and +4 of collections for sale of assets. Moreover, considers payment of leasing, dividend collections&nbsp;&amp; contribution to affiliates. (4)&nbsp;Others include mainly FX differences and net collection
for sale of financial assets. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>4.2 NET DEBT </B></P> <P STYLE="font-size:12pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

<HR SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">


<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">


<IMG SRC="g66804dsp008a.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">As of September&nbsp;30, 2025, YPF&#8217;s <B>consolidated net debt</B> totaled US$9,595&nbsp;million,
increasing by US$762&nbsp;million q/q. The higher net debt resulted in an increase in the <B>net leverage ratio</B> from 1.9x in 2Q25 to 2.1x in 3Q25, mainly due to the financing of shale blocks acquired from Total. Excluding this M&amp;A
transaction, the net leverage ratio proforma would have amounted to 1.9x. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In terms of <B>financing</B>, during 3Q25 YPF tapped the local capital market,
issuing three new bonds: </P> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="4%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="5%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">1)</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman; " ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">US$-MEP</FONT> bond for US$250&nbsp;million, with a <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">2-year</FONT> tenor at 7.5% rate </P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="4%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="5%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">2)</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman; " ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">US$-MEP</FONT> bond for US$51&nbsp;million with a <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">3-year</FONT> tenor at 7.5% rate </P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="4%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="5%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">3)</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman; " ALIGN="left">US$-Cable bond for US$225&nbsp;million with a <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">5-year</FONT> tenor (initial
issuance of US$167&nbsp;million at 8.75% rate, followed by a reopening of US$58&nbsp;million at 8.25% yield) </P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In addition, by the end of
September, YPF secured a US$300&nbsp;million international bridge loan, which, combined with the US$-Cable issuances, allowed to finance the recent acquisition of Total&#8217;s shale assets. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">After 3Q25, we issued in the local market a <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">US$-MEP</FONT> bond for US$99&nbsp;million, with a
<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">15-months</FONT> tenor at 6%. Moreover, in October, we reopened the syndicated cross-border loan market by signing a US$700&nbsp;million export-backed facility with 10 international banks. The loan has a <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">3-year</FONT> tenor and was structured as a prefunding strategy for refinancing local maturities coming due in 1Q26. Lastly, in late October, we successfully returned to the international capital market by <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">re-opening</FONT> our 2031 international bond for US$500&nbsp;million at 8.25% yield. The proceeds will be used to fully repay the bridge loan for the acquisition of Total Austral&#8217;s shale assets and to finance
YPF&#8217;s investment plan. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Additionally, during July, YPF&#8217;s credit rating was upgraded by Moody&#8217;s credit agency, after the upgrade in
sovereign ratings, from Caa1 to B2, maintaining stable outlook. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Regarding our <B>maturity profile</B>, for the last quarter of 2025, the Company faces
US$479&nbsp;million of manageable maturities, mostly local: US$365&nbsp;million of short-term trade facilities with local banks; US$60&nbsp;million of export-backed bond amortizations; and US$54&nbsp;million of local bank loans. In addition, the
Company decided to fully redeem in <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Nov-25</FONT> the last two amortizations of the Secured notes due 2026 (a total of US$120&nbsp;million). </P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>6. ABOUT YPF </B></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">YPF is the
largest energy company in Argentina, fully integrated in the oil and gas value chain. Our main businesses are: (i)&nbsp;in the upstream, we produce +30% of the country&#8217;s oil and gas, and we are the largest shale producer in Vaca Muerta, in
process of divestment of conventional mature fields; (ii)&nbsp;in the downstream, we operate 4 refineries (+50% of Argentina&#8217;s refining capacity) and lead the local diesel and gasoline sales (market share &gt;55%); and (iii)&nbsp;in gas and
power, Metrogas, our subsidiary, distributes ~25% of the country&#8217;s natural gas, while YPF Luz, our affiliate, is the third largest power generation company in Argentina. The Government is the controlling shareholder with a 51% stake, and YPF
is listed in the NYSE and ByMA. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>7. DISCLAIMER </B></P>

found in the &#8220;Investors&#8221; section on the website at <U>www.ypf.com</U>. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">This document does not constitute an offer to sell or the solicitation
